# Glossary {#p-07-glossary-glossary}

This glossary collects the key terms across all nine ECBA domains. It is a study and quick-reference tool, scoped to what the exam tests — not an exhaustive BABOK dictionary.

**How to read an entry.** Each term is followed by a concise definition and, in italics, its *category* and the *activity statement(s)* where it is taught. The activity-statement links take you to teaching sections for the term; a term may also appear elsewhere in the book. Categories include *BACCM concept*, *BABOK task*, *technique*, *competency*, *shared value*, *business analysis principle*, *requirement type*, *traceability relationship*, *risk response*, *Verify quality characteristic*, and *delivery model*.

Two recurring frames worth holding throughout:
- **Scope.** At the ECBA (entry) level the BA *supports, analyzes, recommends, documents, and flags*; decision and design responsibilities depend on the role and agreed governance.
- **The three checks.** *Confirm* = captured right? · *Verify* = usable for further work (quality)? · *Validate* = the right thing (value)?

For related distinctions, see [Appendix C — Concept Pairs](#p-06-appendix-c-concept-pairs-appendix-c-critical-concept-pairs-and-near-miss-distinctions).

---

## A {#p-07-glossary-a}

### Accept {#gl-accept}

*(risk response · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Take a risk knowingly because the expected value is high.

### Acceptance criteria {#gl-acceptance-criteria}

*(A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* The conditions a solution must meet; built through concrete examples (via the "Get real using examples" principle).

### Adaptability {#gl-adaptability}

*(competency · priority five · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* Adjusting your behavioral style to be more effective with different audiences.

### Adaptive approach {#gl-adaptive-approach}

*(approach · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.2](#as-3-2))* Deliver value in small, prioritized increments; share with stakeholders, learn from feedback, and adapt. Uses just-enough, just-in-time documentation. Fits complex, uncertain, emerging needs.

### Ambiguity {#gl-ambiguity}

*(confirmation problem · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Wording allows multiple readings (e.g., "notify the manager quickly").

### Analyze Current State {#gl-analyze-current-state}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [9.2](#as-9-2))* Understanding *why* the enterprise needs to change and *what* will be affected, before a solution is designed; in Domain 9, tested through contextual constraints (regulation, policy, technology limits, structure).

### Analyze Performance Measures {#gl-analyze-performance-measures}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* Interpreting performance data that has *already been collected* — trends, variances, meaning for value. Output: Solution Performance Analysis.

### Analyze Potential Value and Recommend Solution {#gl-analyze-potential-value-and-recommend-solution}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Compares the potential value of options (benefits, costs, risks, uncertainty, alignment) and recommends one.

### Analyze to determine what is valuable {#gl-analyze-to-determine-what-is-valuable}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Continuously assess and prioritize work to maximize value.

### Approach {#gl-approach}

*(A.S. [3.2](#as-3-2))* The intentionally chosen way of doing the analysis and delivery work for a situation (predictive, adaptive, or hybrid). May be imposed; the entry-level BA works within it.

### Approval tracking {#gl-approval-tracking}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* The record of *what* was approved, *by whom*, and *when* — regardless of whether approval was formal or informal.

### Approve Requirements and Designs {#gl-approve-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4), [9.1](#as-9-1))* Obtains and tracks agreement under the roles and process set through governance. Complete agreement is not always necessary; identify and manage the risks of disagreement. Approval does not repair poor quality (BABOK §5.5).

### Assess Enterprise Limitations {#gl-assess-enterprise-limitations}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [8.2](#as-8-2))* Identifies factors *around* the solution (culture, processes, structure, stakeholder interests, technical environment) that block value.

### Assess Requirements Changes {#gl-assess-requirements-changes}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.4](#as-4-4), [6.3](#as-6-3))* Evaluates the implications of a proposed change to requirements/designs across formality, impact, value, scope, schedule, risk, and related requirements. Outputs are *recommendations* to approve, modify, or deny — not the BA approving.

### Assess Solution Limitations {#gl-assess-solution-limitations}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Identifies barriers *inside* the solution that block value.

### Assumptions {#gl-assumptions}

*(A.S. [5.3](#as-5-3))* Beliefs taken as true, made explicit during validation so their risks can be managed.

### Avoid {#gl-avoid}

*(risk response · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Change the approach so the risk is *no longer taken* (the risk is gone). Stays "avoid" even if a different risk appears.

### Avoid waste {#gl-avoid-waste}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Identify value-adding and non-value-adding activities, then remove what does not help satisfy the need.

## B {#p-07-glossary-b}

### BA performance {#gl-ba-performance}

*(A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* How the *analysis work itself* is going (slow reviews, repeated clarifications, rework). The work *about the work*.

### BABOK (Business Analysis Body of Knowledge) {#gl-babok-business-analysis-body-of-knowledge}

*(reference · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* IIBA's larger reference guide; source of the 29 underlying competencies. Not required study for the ECBA.

### BACCM (Business Analysis Core Concept Model) {#gl-baccm-business-analysis-core-concept-model}

*(core model · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2))* A thinking and organizing model built on six interrelated concepts — Change, Need, Solution, Stakeholder, Value, Context. Change any one and the others must be re-checked.

### Backlog {#gl-backlog}

*(A.S. [3.2](#as-3-2))* A prioritized, evolving list of remaining work an adaptive team draws from increment by increment.

### Backlog Management {#gl-backlog-management}

*(technique · A.S. [5.4](#as-5-4))* Record, track, periodically review, and re-prioritize remaining work.

### Balanced Scorecard {#gl-balanced-scorecard}

*(technique · A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* Pushes measurement beyond finance into four perspectives: financial, customer, internal process, learning & growth.

### Baseline {#gl-baseline}

*(A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* Performance at the start, before the change.

### Brainstorming {#gl-brainstorming}

*(technique · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Generate options before narrowing.

### Business analysis {#gl-business-analysis}

*(official definition · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1))* "The practice of enabling change in an enterprise by defining needs and recommending solutions that deliver value to stakeholders within a given context." Not limited to requirements documentation, IT, or project management.

### Business Analysis as a Service (BAaS) {#gl-business-analysis-as-a-service-baas}

*(delivery model · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.1](#as-3-1))* BA work bought from an external/outsourced provider.

### Business Analysis Competency Model {#gl-business-analysis-competency-model}

*(reference · A.S. [3.1](#as-3-1))* A research and reference guide for developing BA skills, available to anyone who performs the work. **Not** the ECBA blueprint and **not** a certification syllabus.

### Business analysis principles {#gl-business-analysis-principles}

*(framework · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* The seven rules that guide how a BA acts when several reasonable choices exist.

### Business Capability Analysis {#gl-business-capability-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1))* Describes *what the enterprise is able to do*, exposes capability gaps, and supports investment decisions.

### Business Objectives {#gl-business-objectives}

*(Define Future State output · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* One of its three outputs. (Cf. *objective* for the concept.)

### Business Rules Analysis {#gl-business-rules-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [9.3](#as-9-3))* Expresses constraints, including technology constraints, as explicit, visible, traceable rules (e.g., "customer data must be encrypted before exchange").

### Business requirement {#gl-business-requirement}

*(requirement type · A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A goal, objective, or outcome — the *why* of the change.

### Business Acumen {#gl-business-acumen}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Business Acumen](#comp-9-3-1) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Business Analysis Tools and Technology {#gl-business-analysis-tools-and-technology}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Business Analysis Tools and Technology](#comp-9-6-2) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## C {#p-07-glossary-c}

### Capability gap {#gl-capability-gap}

*(A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1))* A missing ability the analysis surfaces (e.g., a retailer can process orders but cannot yet handle returns automatically).

### Capability map {#gl-capability-map}

*(A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1))* A picture of the enterprise's abilities, used to spot gaps and guide investment (output of Business Capability Analysis).

### Centre of Excellence (CoE) {#gl-centre-of-excellence-coe}

*(delivery model · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.1](#as-3-1))* A dedicated internal unit that owns standards, templates, methods, and quality, often with a governance role. The only model with authority to set rules.

### Change {#gl-change}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2); anchors Domain 4)* The act of transformation in response to a need. Not "change management" as a separate discipline.

### Change control (business analysis) {#gl-change-control-business-analysis}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Managing changes to business analysis information — especially requirements and designs — including what is requested, analyzed, documented, communicated, and authorized. Not just project schedule/budget control.

### Change Strategy {#gl-change-strategy}

*(output · A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* The recommended route across the gap from current to future state. One of the two outputs of Define Change Strategy.

### Cohesion {#gl-cohesion}

*(supplementary review term · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* How well a set belongs to one concern. It is not a named characteristic in BABOK §7.2; the BABOK characteristic Concise addresses unnecessary content.

### Collaboration {#gl-collaboration}

*(A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2), [7.3](#as-7-3))* Working toward a common goal — not forcing complete agreement on every detail. As a *shared value* (D2) it is the belief that stakeholders work together to improve understanding and outcomes; as an *engagement concept* (D7) it means sustaining productive joint work toward the shared outcome.

### Collaborative Games {#gl-collaborative-games}

*(technique · A.S. [7.3](#as-7-3))* A light structured activity to surface *hidden* assumptions, unspoken concerns, and tacit knowledge.

### Communicate Business Analysis Information {#gl-communicate-business-analysis-information}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [7.1](#as-7-1), [8.3](#as-8-3))* Sharing assembled information so stakeholders *understand* it, can respond, and can use it — shared understanding, not mere delivery. The D8 angle is communicating value and goal-fit.

### Communication competencies {#gl-communication-competencies}

*(A.S. [7.1](#as-7-1))* Verbal, written, and non-verbal skills that support shared understanding (including reading a puzzled face, not just delivering the slide).

### Community of Practice (CoP) {#gl-community-of-practice-cop}

*(delivery model · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.1](#as-3-1))* An informal, voluntary network sharing experience and good practices; no authority to set rules.

### Completeness {#gl-completeness}

*(Verify quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Nothing needed is missing. Trigger: a gap in the set.

### Atomic {#gl-atomic}

*(BABOK quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Self-contained and understandable independently.

### Concise {#gl-concise}

*(BABOK quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Contains no unnecessary material.

### Unambiguous {#gl-unambiguous}

*(BABOK quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Makes it clear whether the need is fulfilled.

### Prioritized {#gl-prioritized}

*(BABOK quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Has an agreed relative importance or value.

### Understandable {#gl-understandable}

*(BABOK quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Uses terminology familiar to the intended audience.

### Concern {#gl-concern}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* What a stakeholder fears losing, harming, or risking; causes hesitation.

### Conduct Elicitation {#gl-conduct-elicitation}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [7.1](#as-7-1))* The active gathering itself: the BA guides stakeholders, keeps the activity aligned to its goal, and captures outcomes. Output: Elicitation Results (Unconfirmed).

### Confirm Elicitation Results {#gl-confirm-elicitation-results}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Checks whether captured information is *accurate and consistent* with other information ("captured right?"). Output: Elicitation Results (Confirmed).

### Conflict {#gl-conflict}

*(confirmation problem · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Two sources contradict each other (e.g., policy says 30 days, notes say 90).

### Consistency {#gl-consistency}

*(Verify quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* No internal contradictions. Trigger: two requirements conflict with each other.

### Constraint {#gl-constraint}

*(A.S. [9.2](#as-9-2))* A known, fixed boundary that shapes what can be done. Types: technical, organizational, regulatory, budget, time.

### Context {#gl-context}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2); anchors Domain 9)* The circumstances that influence, are influenced by, and provide understanding of the change (culture, regulations, market, technology). Broader than the project boundary, and dynamic — not static.

### Continuous learning {#gl-continuous-learning}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Using feedback and evidence to improve understanding.

### Correctness {#gl-correctness}

*(general quality term · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Accuracy against the relevant source or need. It is not a named characteristic in the nine-item BABOK §7.2 list. Consistent includes alignment with stakeholder needs as well as avoidance of conflicts.

### Courage {#gl-courage}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Raising difficult facts, assumptions, or risks even when uncomfortable.

### Cover {#gl-cover}

*(supplementary coverage term · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A group of lower-level items accounts for a higher-level need. This is a completeness check, rather than an additional named relationship in BABOK §5.1, which names Derive, Depends, Satisfy, and Validate.

### Creative Thinking {#gl-creative-thinking}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* Generating new ideas and alternatives. Testable but not one of the five priority competencies.

### Curiosity {#gl-curiosity}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Asking questions and exploring beyond the first answer; treating a request as a starting point, not the final need.

### Current state {#gl-current-state}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Why change is needed and what will be affected as things stand today.

### Customer focus {#gl-customer-focus}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Considering the real customer experience.

### Conceptual Thinking {#gl-conceptual-thinking}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Conceptual Thinking](#comp-9-1-6) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Communication Tools and Technology {#gl-communication-tools-and-technology}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Communication Tools and Technology](#comp-9-6-3) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## D {#p-07-glossary-d}

### Data Modelling {#gl-data-modelling}

*(technique · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Represents data objects, attributes, and relationships. Recognition-level for the ECBA.

### Decision factor {#gl-decision-factor}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* The reason behind a stakeholder's support, hesitation, or resistance.

### Decision Making {#gl-decision-making}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* Comparing options against agreed criteria to recommend one. Testable but **not** one of the five priority competencies.

### Decision-making authority {#gl-decision-making-authority}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* Who can approve, and under what conditions.

### Define Change Strategy {#gl-define-change-strategy}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Develops and assesses alternative routes for the change and recommends one; its engine is gap analysis; produces the Change Strategy and the Solution Scope.

### Define Design Options {#gl-define-design-options}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Identifies candidate ways to satisfy the need (create, purchase, combination) and compares trade-offs.

### Define Future State {#gl-define-future-state}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2), [8.1](#as-8-1))* Describes the necessary conditions for meeting the need (not an implementation plan). Produces **three** outputs: Business Objectives, Future State Description, Potential Value.

### Define Requirements Architecture {#gl-define-requirements-architecture}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3), [9.3](#as-9-3))* Organizes requirements, interfaces, dependencies, and stakeholder views into a coherent, complete whole that supports the objectives.

### Depends {#gl-depends}

*(traceability relationship · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A necessity dependency makes another requirement necessary for implementation; an effort dependency makes it easier to implement (BABOK §5.1).

### Derive {#gl-derive}

*(traceability relationship · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A lower-level requirement is spawned from a broader one (top-down generation).

### Design {#gl-design}

*(A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A usable representation of a *solution* (how value might be realized if built). Distinguished from a requirement by **purpose, not format**.

### Design constraint {#gl-design-constraint}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* A limit on what the solution itself must (or must not) be (e.g., must integrate with the existing ERP).

### Design idea {#gl-design-idea}

*(A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A stakeholder's solution-shaped request (a specific screen, mechanism, or layout). Treated as a starting point to explore the underlying need, not recorded as the requirement.

### Design options {#gl-design-options}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Candidate ways to satisfy the need and reach the future state: create (build new), purchase (buy/adopt), or a combination. Same as "solution options." Lowest cost is not automatically best.

### Dispersed delivery (dispersed across business units) {#gl-dispersed-delivery-dispersed-across-business-units}

*(delivery model · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.1](#as-3-1))* BA work embedded inside each department, with no central home.

### Do nothing {#gl-do-nothing}

*(recommendation type · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* A legitimate recommendation when the change's value is too low or its risk too high. Recommending no change *with evidence* is judgment, not surrender.

### Document Analysis {#gl-document-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1), [9.1](#as-9-1))* Elicitation by reviewing existing sources for background or to compare against what stakeholders say; supports keeping information usable when it is scattered.

### Domain SME {#gl-domain-sme}

*(stakeholder role · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Knows the business area, policy, process, or need.

### Driver {#gl-driver}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* What a stakeholder hopes to gain; pulls toward support.

## E {#p-07-glossary-e}

### Elicitation Activity Plan {#gl-elicitation-activity-plan}

*(output · A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1))* The output of Prepare for Elicitation.

### Elicitation Results (Unconfirmed) {#gl-elicitation-results-unconfirmed}

*(A.S. [7.1](#as-7-1))* The raw output of Conduct Elicitation, before it has been checked for accuracy or consistency.

### Empowering mindset {#gl-empowering-mindset}

*(mindset · A.S. [2.1](#as-2-1))* A flexible, context-aware, deliberately learning mindset that reads the situation and adapts, rather than reusing what worked before.

### Enabled abilities {#gl-enabled-abilities}

*(framework · A.S. [2.1](#as-2-1))* The seven things an empowering mindset lets you do (outputs the mindset produces): create quality outcomes, uncover value, prioritize focus, empathize and learn, build allies, adapt to feedback, simplify knowledge.

### End User {#gl-end-user}

*(stakeholder role · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Directly uses the solution or takes part in the process.

### Enterprise {#gl-enterprise}

*(A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1))* The setting for change: a whole organization, part of one, or a broader system of organizations.

### Enterprise limitation {#gl-enterprise-limitation}

*(A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [8.2](#as-8-2), [9.2](#as-9-2))* A barrier *around* the solution (culture, incentives, process, structure, adoption, infrastructure) that blocks value even when the solution works as designed.

### Error {#gl-error}

*(confirmation problem · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Something captured is wrong (notes say "optional"; the stakeholder said mandatory).

### Ethical Analysis {#gl-ethical-analysis}

*(organizational consideration · A.S. [3.3](#as-3-3))* Ensuring the work is right and fair, not merely legal or fast — especially where automated systems or AI affect people.

### Ethics {#gl-ethics}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Considering whether the work is right and fair — not just legal or fast. Broader than legal compliance.

### Evolutionary change {#gl-evolutionary-change}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Incremental improvements over time. Describes the *scale and nature* of a change, not the delivery approach.

### Evolve together {#gl-evolve-together}

*(A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* Requirements and designs are interdependent and cyclical, not a one-way pipeline: a design can reveal a new requirement; a changed requirement can force a design to be revisited.

## F {#p-07-glossary-f}

### Facilitation {#gl-facilitation}

*(competency · priority five · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4), [7.3](#as-7-3))* Structuring discussions and group interaction **neutrally** toward productive outcomes, without taking a position.

### Feasibility {#gl-feasibility}

*(Verify quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Implementable within constraints. Trigger: unrealistic given budget, time, technology, or risk.

### Financial value {#gl-financial-value}

*(value dimension · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Revenue, cost reduction, ROI, avoided cost.

### Focus areas {#gl-focus-areas}

*(framework · A.S. [2.1](#as-2-1))* The six things a BA deliberately works on to develop an empowering mindset: achieve outcomes, discover value, adopt principles, decide the approach, build competencies, use techniques.

### Formal approval {#gl-formal-approval}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* A defined governance process with documented sign-off.

### Foundational competencies {#gl-foundational-competencies}

*(framework · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* The basic skills, abilities, and characteristics that make business analysis effective. Domain 2 highlights five as important for any initiative.

### Functional requirement {#gl-functional-requirement}

*(requirement type · A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A solution requirement: a capability the solution must have — behavior or information it handles (e.g., "the system displays delayed orders by zone").

### Future state {#gl-future-state}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* The conditions that satisfy the business need.

### Future State Description {#gl-future-state-description}

*(Define Future State output · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* One of its three outputs.

## G {#p-07-glossary-g}

### Gap analysis {#gl-gap-analysis}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Comparing the current state with the future state to see what is missing: capabilities, processes, systems, data, structures, or technology.

### Get real using examples {#gl-get-real-using-examples}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Use concrete examples to build shared understanding and support acceptance criteria, design, and testing.

### Goal (business goal) {#gl-goal-business-goal}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2), [8.1](#as-8-1))* A broad, directional, qualitative intent, with no measure or timeframe ("become the preferred digital channel"; "improve customer satisfaction").

## H {#p-07-glossary-h}

### Hybrid approach {#gl-hybrid-approach}

*(approach · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.2](#as-3-2))* Predictive and adaptive used together when parts of one initiative genuinely need different approaches. Not "the average" of the two and not a default hedge.

## I {#p-07-glossary-i}

### Identify Business Analysis Performance Improvements {#gl-identify-business-analysis-performance-improvements}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* Assesses how the BA work is going (planned vs. actual), finds root causes of gaps, and suggests improvements.

### Impact {#gl-impact}

*(A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* How much the change *affects* a stakeholder — "what happens *to* them."

### Implementation constraint {#gl-implementation-constraint}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* A limit on how the solution can be built or delivered: time, budget, resources, delivery (e.g., budget ≤ $500k; ship in 6 months).

### Implementation SME {#gl-implementation-sme}

*(stakeholder role · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Knows how solution components can be built.

### Improvement {#gl-improvement}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Looking for better ways to work and better outcomes.

### Inclusion {#gl-inclusion}

*(A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Actively seeking diverse stakeholder perspectives to reduce blind spots; a practical part of Ethics.

### Inclusion and Representation {#gl-inclusion-and-representation}

*(organizational consideration · A.S. [3.3](#as-3-3))* Actively seeking and amplifying diverse perspectives so they actually shape decisions — more than token representation.

### Indicator {#gl-indicator}

*(A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* The thing a metric quantifies (a metric is a quantifiable level of an indicator).

### Influence {#gl-influence}

*(A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* A stakeholder's ability to *affect* the change — "what they can *make* happen."

### Informal approval {#gl-informal-approval}

*(A.S. [7.4](#as-7-4))* A verbal "yes" or lightweight confirmation, where the governance approach permits it.

### Information management approach {#gl-information-management-approach}

*(A.S. [6.4](#as-6-4), [9.1](#as-9-1))* How BA information is organized, located, understood, maintained, protected, and reused — plus abstraction level, traceability, and attributes. Not just buying a tool/repository.

### Intangible value {#gl-intangible-value}

*(form of value · A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* Value measured indirectly, often perception- or motivation-based (morale, reputation, customer trust, stakeholder confidence).

### Interest {#gl-interest}

*(A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* What a stakeholder cares about; the stake they hold.

### Interface requirements {#gl-interface-requirements}

*(A.S. [9.3](#as-9-3))* Requirements relating to system-to-system data exchanges.

### Interviews {#gl-interviews}

*(technique · A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1), [7.4](#as-7-4))* Elicitation for depth, clarification, privacy, or one stakeholder's perspective; room for follow-up.

### Industry Knowledge {#gl-industry-knowledge}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Industry Knowledge](#comp-9-3-2) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## J {#p-07-glossary-j}

### Just enough, just in time {#gl-just-enough-just-in-time}

*(A.S. [3.2](#as-3-2))* The adaptive documentation stance: right-sized for the current increment, changing *when and how much* you document — not *whether*. Right-sized is not zero.

## K {#p-07-glossary-k}

### KPI (Key Performance Indicator) {#gl-kpi-key-performance-indicator}

*(A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3), [8.4](#as-8-4))* A metric tied to a strategic goal or objective. All KPIs are metrics; not all metrics are KPIs.

## L {#p-07-glossary-l}

### Lagging indicator {#gl-lagging-indicator}

*(A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* Measures results already achieved; confirms value was realized (after the fact).

### Leadership and Influencing {#gl-leadership-and-influencing}

*(competency · priority five · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4), [7.2](#as-7-2))* Building consensus and guiding stakeholders toward value **without** formal authority.

### Leading indicator {#gl-leading-indicator}

*(A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* Predicts future performance; gives early warning and supports course-correction.

### Lessons Learned {#gl-lessons-learned}

*(technique · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* Captures qualitative retrospective insight: what went well, what didn't, what to change next time.

### Level of abstraction {#gl-level-of-abstraction}

*(A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* The level of detail at which a requirement is represented; must fit the audience and purpose.

### Logistics {#gl-logistics}

*(A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1))* The practical setup of an elicitation activity: participants, roles, resources, location, schedule, channels, tools.

### Learning {#gl-learning}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Learning](#comp-9-1-3) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Listening {#gl-listening}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Listening](#comp-9-4-4) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## M {#p-07-glossary-m}

### Maintain Requirements and Designs {#gl-maintain-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [6.4](#as-6-4), [9.1](#as-9-1))* Retains accuracy and consistency throughout and beyond the change and enables reuse. Includes content, relationships, and attributes; approved changes are reflected in the maintained information (BABOK §5.2).

### Manage Stakeholder Collaboration {#gl-manage-stakeholder-collaboration}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [7.3](#as-7-3))* Gain agreement on commitments, monitor engagement, keep communication bi-directional, and handle obstacles without damaging relationships. Output: Stakeholder Engagement.

### Market value {#gl-market-value}

*(value dimension · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Competitive position or a new opportunity.

### Measurable evaluation criteria {#gl-measurable-evaluation-criteria}

*(A.S. [5.3](#as-5-3))* How we'll know the change worked; defined during validation.

### Measure Solution Performance {#gl-measure-solution-performance}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* Defines and collects the performance measures in the first place. Output: Solution Performance Measures.

### Methodology Knowledge {#gl-methodology-knowledge}

*(competency · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Learn the organization's current approach and align to it; don't replace it with a preferred prior method.

### Metric {#gl-metric}

*(A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3), [8.4](#as-8-4))* A quantifiable level of an indicator; any number you track. Becomes a KPI only when tied to a strategic goal/objective.

### Metrics and KPIs {#gl-metrics-and-kpis}

*(technique · A.S. [8.1](#as-8-1), [8.4](#as-8-4))* Defining measures of performance and value.

### Mindset {#gl-mindset}

*(definition · A.S. [2.1](#as-2-1))* The mental framework that guides decision-making, behavior, and team dynamics.

### Mitigate {#gl-mitigate}

*(risk response · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Reduce the probability or impact of a risk you are *still taking* (e.g., pilot before rollout).

### Modifiability {#gl-modifiability}

*(supplementary checklist term · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Ease of making changes without excessive ripple effects. Use it when a local review explicitly includes it; it is not one of the nine named BABOK §7.2 characteristics.

## N {#p-07-glossary-n}

### Need {#gl-need}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2); anchors Domain 5)* A problem or opportunity to be addressed. Not a requirement and not a stakeholder's proposed feature.

### Negotiation and Conflict Resolution {#gl-negotiation-and-conflict-resolution}

*(technique / competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4), [7.4](#as-7-4))* Helping parties with **competing interests** find common ground; separating interests from positions without damaging relationships.

### Nine functions of business analysis {#gl-nine-functions-of-business-analysis}

*(framework · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1))* The value-creating jobs of BA, clustered as: understand the problem; shape a value-focused solution; deliver and keep improving. Recognize the pattern; verbatim recall not required.

### Non-functional requirement {#gl-non-functional-requirement}

*(requirement type · A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A solution requirement: a quality or condition the solution must meet — how *well* it works (speed, availability, security), not what it does. Still a requirement, not a design.

### Non-Verbal Communication {#gl-non-verbal-communication}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Non-Verbal Communication](#comp-9-4-2) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## O {#p-07-glossary-o}

### Objective (business objective) {#gl-objective-business-objective}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2), [8.1](#as-8-1))* A specific, measurable, time-bound target with a baseline, target, measure, or deadline ("raise the CSAT score from 72 to 82 by Q4").

### Omission {#gl-omission}

*(confirmation problem · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Something important was discussed but never captured in the notes.

### Organizational considerations {#gl-organizational-considerations}

*(framework · A.S. [3.3](#as-3-3))* Four organization-wide factors that shape how analysis is done: security, ethics, inclusion, sustainability.

### Orphan component {#gl-orphan-component}

*(A.S. [6.1](#as-6-1))* A solution component with *no supporting requirement* — may indicate scope creep or an unsupported design decision. Record and raise.

### Orphan requirement {#gl-orphan-requirement}

*(A.S. [6.1](#as-6-1))* A requirement with *no design, component, or test* — an unaddressed need. Record and raise.

### Outcome {#gl-outcome}

*(A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* What changed because of an output (faster decisions, fewer errors, higher trust). Value is judged by outcomes, not delivery.

### Output {#gl-output}

*(A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* What was produced or delivered (dashboard, report, portal, process).

### Organizational Modelling {#gl-organizational-modelling}

*(technique · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* See [Organizational Modelling](#tech-10-32) in [Appendix A](#p-04-appendix-a-techniques-appendix-a-ecba-techniques-quick-reference) for its purpose and usage considerations.

### Organization and Time Management {#gl-organization-and-time-management}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Organization and Time Management](#comp-9-2-4) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Organization Knowledge {#gl-organization-knowledge}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Organization Knowledge](#comp-9-3-3) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Office Productivity Tools and Technology {#gl-office-productivity-tools-and-technology}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Office Productivity Tools and Technology](#comp-9-6-1) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## P {#p-07-glossary-p}

### Performance enhancement {#gl-performance-enhancement}

*(BA function · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [1.3](#as-1-3))* Continuing to monitor outcomes and improvement opportunities *after* the solution is live; BA work does not stop at implementation.

### Persona {#gl-persona}

*(stakeholder format · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* A detailed profile of a representative user type: goals, needs, constraints, behaviors.

### Plan Business Analysis Approach {#gl-plan-business-analysis-approach}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [9.4](#as-9-4))* Fitting *how* the analysis is done to the situation — rigor, structure, communication. Standards, frameworks, policies, regulation, and risk level shape its formality and governance.

### Plan Business Analysis Governance {#gl-plan-business-analysis-governance}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* Defines how decisions about requirements and designs get made: reviews, change control, prioritization, approvals, and what information a decision-maker needs.

### Plan Business Analysis Information Management {#gl-plan-business-analysis-information-management}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [6.4](#as-6-4))* Defines the information management approach.

### Plan Stakeholder Engagement {#gl-plan-stakeholder-engagement}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Identifies stakeholders (internal and external) and plans engagement. Output: Stakeholder Engagement Approach.

### Potential value {#gl-potential-value}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2), [8.2](#as-8-2))* Value estimated *before* a solution is fully realized; carries uncertainty; spans financial, reputational, and market dimensions. Also one of the three outputs of Define Future State.

### Predictive approach {#gl-predictive-approach}

*(approach · A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1), [3.2](#as-3-2))* Start with a plan; identify needed information up front, then work the plan and track against it. More formal, up-front documentation. Fits well-defined, regulated, sequential work.

### Prepare for Elicitation {#gl-prepare-for-elicitation}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1))* Getting ready before a session: understand scope, select techniques, set up logistics, secure supporting material, prepare stakeholders. Output: Elicitation Activity Plan.

### Prioritization bases {#gl-prioritization-bases}

*(A.S. [5.4](#as-5-4))* The grounds on which needs are ranked: business value/benefit, time sensitivity/urgency, dependencies, regulatory/policy compliance, risk, cost/effort.

### Priority five competencies {#gl-priority-five-competencies}

*(A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* The five foundational competencies the Standard singles out for any initiative: Adaptability, Facilitation, Leadership and Influencing, Problem Solving, Systems Thinking.

### Problem Solving {#gl-problem-solving}

*(competency · priority five · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* Ensuring the value created addresses the **root cause** of a problem, not the symptom.

### Process Analysis {#gl-process-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [6.1](#as-6-1))* Surfaces workflow issues: bottlenecks, unnecessary handoffs, missed steps, duplicated work.

### Process Modelling {#gl-process-modelling}

*(technique · A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3))* Shows activity flow, handoffs, and implementation-related process impacts.

### Personal Accountability {#gl-personal-accountability}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Personal Accountability](#comp-9-2-2) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## R {#p-07-glossary-r}

### Recommend Actions to Increase Solution Value {#gl-recommend-actions-to-increase-solution-value}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Recommends actions when value is blocked: do nothing, organizational change, reduce solution complexity, eliminate redundancy, avoid waste, retire the solution.

### Regulator {#gl-regulator}

*(stakeholder role · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Defines or enforces standards, legislation, audit, or compliance.

### Relay (active translation) {#gl-relay-active-translation}

*(A.S. [8.3](#as-8-3))* Interpreting and presenting information so a specific audience can use it — changing format, terminology, and detail, never the truth. Not passive forwarding, and not dumbing down to a claim with no mechanism.

### Reporting {#gl-reporting}

*(A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* Communicating measures at defined intervals.

### Reputational value {#gl-reputational-value}

*(value dimension · A.S. [8.2](#as-8-2))* Trust, credibility, brand perception.

### Requirement {#gl-requirement}

*(A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A usable representation of a *need* (what value could be delivered if fulfilled). Distinguished from a design by **purpose, not format**.

### Requirement attributes {#gl-requirement-attributes}

*(A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Metadata about a requirement (ID, priority, status, owner, source) that support management and traceability.

### Requirements architecture {#gl-requirements-architecture}

*(A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3), [9.3](#as-9-3))* The organization of requirements so they cohere and are complete as a set (by stakeholder perspective, dependency, completeness, views). Not technical solution architecture.

### Respect {#gl-respect}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Stakeholder input is heard and considered.

### Risk {#gl-risk}

*(A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2), [9.2](#as-9-2))* Uncertainty and its effect on value; an uncertain event and its effect.

### Risk Analysis and Management {#gl-risk-analysis-and-management}

*(technique · A.S. [5.4](#as-5-4), [6.2](#as-6-2), [9.2](#as-9-2))* Compare and manage by probability, impact, tolerance, and response. A constraint often *creates* a risk (a deadline is a constraint; missing it is the risk).

### Role-agnostic / industry-agnostic {#gl-role-agnostic-industry-agnostic}

*(A.S. [1.1](#as-1-1))* BA is not tied to a job title or sector; anyone applying BA concepts, practices, and tasks in any industry may be performing business analysis.

### Root Cause Analysis {#gl-root-cause-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3), [4.4](#as-4-4), [5.3](#as-5-3))* Finds the underlying cause of a problem rather than its symptom, so a fix addresses the real issue.

## S {#p-07-glossary-s}

### Satisfy {#gl-satisfy}

*(traceability relationship · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A solution *component* satisfies (implements) a requirement.

### Scope (of an elicitation activity) {#gl-scope-of-an-elicitation-activity}

*(A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1))* The business domain, expected outputs, and stakeholder dynamics (who is involved and how they interact).

### Scope Modelling {#gl-scope-modelling}

*(technique · A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3))* Visualizes solution boundaries, in/out-of-scope elements, and external interfaces.

### Security Integration {#gl-security-integration}

*(organizational consideration · A.S. [3.3](#as-3-3))* Security belongs in analysis, not only after build; the BA flags risks and brings in security expertise when needed.

### See the whole {#gl-see-the-whole}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Analyze needs in the big-picture context and understand *why* a change is necessary.

### Shared values {#gl-shared-values}

*(framework · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* The beliefs that keep business analysis useful, ethical, and value-focused; recognized as behaviors (nine in the Standard): Respect, Courage, Collaboration, Ethics, Curiosity, Continuous learning, Improvement, Customer focus, Value maximization.

### Solution {#gl-solution}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2); anchors Domain 6)* A specific way of satisfying one or more needs in a context.

### Solution architecture {#gl-solution-architecture}

*(A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3), [9.3](#as-9-3))* The design of the technical system and how its parts interact (APIs, schemas, microservices, platforms, protocols). **Not** ECBA-level BA work.

### Solution limitation {#gl-solution-limitation}

*(A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [8.2](#as-8-2), [9.2](#as-9-2))* A barrier *inside* the solution (a defect, missing function, poor performance, usability).

### Solution performance {#gl-solution-performance}

*(A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A live solution's measured data: processing time, complaint volume, adoption rate.

### Solution Scope {#gl-solution-scope}

*(output · A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* The boundary of what the change will achieve. An *output* of Define Change Strategy — not an input.

### Solution validation {#gl-solution-validation}

*(concept · A.S. [6.1](#as-6-1))* Checking whether the solution aligns with the business need and expected value ("are we building the right thing?"). Not software or user-acceptance testing.

### Specify and Model Requirements and Designs {#gl-specify-and-model-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* Turns confirmed results into clear, usable requirements and designs (text, matrices, diagrams) at the right level of abstraction.

### Sponsor {#gl-sponsor}

*(stakeholder role · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Authorizes the work; controls budget/scope; may approve key information.

### Stakeholder {#gl-stakeholder}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2); anchors Domain 7)* A group or individual with a relationship to the change, the need, or the solution — internal or external, not just the project team or approvers.

### Stakeholder Engagement {#gl-stakeholder-engagement}

*(output · A.S. [7.3](#as-7-3))* Genuine willingness to participate; attendance alone isn't engagement.

### Stakeholder Engagement Approach {#gl-stakeholder-engagement-approach}

*(output · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* The engagement *strategy*: how much each stakeholder is involved, which technique fits whom, engagement risks, and expected feedback. Broader than a contact list or comms plan.

### Stakeholder List, Map, or Personas {#gl-stakeholder-list-map-or-personas}

*(technique · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Three formats — list (a simple inventory), map/matrix (analyze standing), persona (a user-type profile).

### Stakeholder map/matrix {#gl-stakeholder-map-matrix}

*(stakeholder format · A.S. [7.2](#as-7-2))* Plots stakeholders to analyze standing (power, interest, influence, impact) and target engagement.

### Stakeholder requirement {#gl-stakeholder-requirement}

*(requirement type · A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* What a stakeholder group needs from the solution; bridges business and solution requirements (e.g., "supervisors need visibility into delayed tasks").

### Stimulate collaboration and continuous improvement {#gl-stimulate-collaboration-and-continuous-improvement}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Create conditions where stakeholders contribute and feedback improves the work.

### Strategic / tactical / operational levels {#gl-strategic-tactical-operational-levels}

*(A.S. [1.3](#as-1-3))* The three levels at which BA supports value: long-term direction (strategic), chosen initiatives and trade-offs (tactical), day-to-day work and performance (operational). BA helps keep them aligned.

### Sustainability Practices {#gl-sustainability-practices}

*(organizational consideration · A.S. [3.3](#as-3-3))* Weighing current needs against future resources — environmental, social-equity, health, and economic viability.

### SWOT Analysis {#gl-swot-analysis}

*(technique · A.S. [4.1](#as-4-1), [8.2](#as-8-2))* Sorts factors into strengths and weaknesses (internal) against opportunities and threats (external); supports spotting value opportunities and threats.

### Systems Thinking {#gl-systems-thinking}

*(competency · priority five · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* Understanding the enterprise and context **holistically** (process, policy, technology, people, impacts together) to maximize value.

### Solution Knowledge {#gl-solution-knowledge}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Solution Knowledge](#comp-9-3-4) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## T {#p-07-glossary-t}

### Tailoring decisions {#gl-tailoring-decisions}

*(A.S. [7.1](#as-7-1))* The five communication choices: information, timing, format, terminology, audience.

### Tangible value {#gl-tangible-value}

*(form of value · A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* Directly measurable value, often with a monetary component (revenue increase, cost reduction, faster processing, fewer errors).

### Target {#gl-target}

*(A.S. [8.4](#as-8-4))* The intended level of a measure.

### Teamwork {#gl-teamwork}

*(competency · A.S. [7.3](#as-7-3))* Supports shared goals, trust, and productive conflict.

### Testability {#gl-testability}

*(Verify quality characteristic · A.S. [9.4](#as-9-4))* Can be verified through testing or analysis. Trigger: vague, unmeasurable wording.

### Think as a customer {#gl-think-as-a-customer}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Understand the customer experience and break it into usable detail.

### Trace Requirements and Designs {#gl-trace-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3), [6.1](#as-6-1))* Keeps requirements and designs aligned across levels and reveals the ripple of a change; uses the five relationships to detect solution gaps (orphan components/requirements, workflow gaps). Also called Trace Requirements.

### Traceability {#gl-traceability}

*(A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4); recurs in D4, D6)* Tracking relationships from the original need *forward* to solution components and *backward* from a component to the need it serves — bidirectionally. Exposes a component with no stated need, or a need with nothing built for it.

### Transfer {#gl-transfer}

*(risk response · A.S. [6.2](#as-6-2))* Shift responsibility/exposure to an *external* party (insurer, vendor). Re-assigning internally is not transfer.

### Transformational change {#gl-transformational-change}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* A large-scale shift in how the enterprise operates. Describes the *scale and nature* of a change, not the delivery approach.

### Transition requirement {#gl-transition-requirement}

*(requirement type · A.S. [3.4](#as-3-4))* A *temporary* capability needed only to move from the current to the future state (data conversion, training, cutover). Falls away after go-live.

### Transition state {#gl-transition-state}

*(A.S. [4.2](#as-4-2))* An intermediate, temporary stage between the current and future state, reached using transition requirements.

### Trustworthiness {#gl-trustworthiness}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Trustworthiness](#comp-9-2-3) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Teaching {#gl-teaching}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Teaching](#comp-9-5-5) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## U {#p-07-glossary-u}

### Understand what is doable {#gl-understand-what-is-doable}

*(business analysis principle · A.S. [2.3](#as-2-3))* Consider constraints and the operating environment so the solution can deliver value.

### User Stories {#gl-user-stories}

*(technique · A.S. [5.2](#as-5-2))* A short, value-focused way to express what a user needs and why. Recognition-level for the ECBA.

## V {#p-07-glossary-v}

### Validate {#gl-validate}

*(traceability relationship · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* A test case or check determines whether the built solution fulfils a requirement. (Distinct from the *Validate Requirements and Designs* task.)

### Validate Requirements and Designs {#gl-validate-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [5.3](#as-5-3), [8.3](#as-8-3), [9.4](#as-9-4))* Checks alignment to business goals and the delivery of needed value ("is it the right thing?"). Output: Requirements (Validated).

### Value {#gl-value}

*(BACCM concept · A.S. [1.2](#as-1-2), [1.4](#as-1-4); anchors Domain 8)* The worth, importance, or usefulness of something to a stakeholder within a context. Can be tangible or intangible, realized or preserved, and is relative to stakeholder and context.

### Value maximization {#gl-value-maximization}

*(shared value · A.S. [2.2](#as-2-2))* Helping focus effort on the greatest value in context.

### Value preserved {#gl-value-preserved}

*(A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* Value created by reducing or avoiding losses, risks, or costs (e.g., a compliance change that lowers penalty risk).

### Value realized {#gl-value-realized}

*(A.S. [1.4](#as-1-4))* Value created through new returns, gains, or improvements (e.g., a new flow that lifts completed applications).

### Verify Requirements and Designs {#gl-verify-requirements-and-designs}

*(BABOK task · A.S. [5.3](#as-5-3), [9.4](#as-9-4))* Checks whether requirements and designs are usable for further work. BABOK §7.2 names Atomic, Complete, Consistent, Concise, Feasible, Unambiguous, Testable, Prioritized, and Understandable. Checks are repeated as information develops.

### View {#gl-view}

*(A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3))* The actual requirements produced using a viewpoint.

### Viewpoint {#gl-viewpoint}

*(A.S. [6.3](#as-6-3))* A set of conventions for representing requirements from a perspective.

### Visual Thinking {#gl-visual-thinking}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Visual Thinking](#comp-9-1-7) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

### Verbal Communication {#gl-verbal-communication}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Verbal Communication](#comp-9-4-1) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.

## W {#p-07-glossary-w}

### Workflow gap {#gl-workflow-gap}

*(A.S. [6.1](#as-6-1))* A process step nothing in the solution supports.

### Working under direction {#gl-working-under-direction}

*(role context · A.S. [4.3](#as-4-3))* Perform assigned analysis and recommendations within the responsibilities specified in the scenario. This phrase does not impose a universal ban on decision authority for every person with an entry-level certification.

### Workshops {#gl-workshops}

*(technique · A.S. [5.1](#as-5-1), [7.3](#as-7-3))* A facilitated session with a defined goal and work product; elicitation for shared understanding, consensus, or cross-functional alignment.

### Written Communication {#gl-written-communication}

*(competency · A.S. [2.4](#as-2-4))* See [Written Communication](#comp-9-4-3) in [Appendix B](#p-05-appendix-b-competencies-appendix-b-competency-quick-reference) for its purpose, meaning, and effectiveness measures.
